========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. I really appreciate it, and I hope I'm not taking up too much of your time with this. 

I'm here because... well, it's probably just me being overly sensitive, but I've been having a bit of a hard time lately. I'm an artist, and recently I was working on a memorial piece for my aunt. And, um... I just kept getting these really uncomfortable, unwanted thoughts while I was putting it together. 

It sounds silly to say out loud, but I kept thinking that if I made one wrong color choice, or if a photo placement was just slightly off, it meant I was going to dishonor her somehow... or that it proved I didn't actually love her enough. I tried to just brush them off as normal creative doubts—because artists get those all the time, right?—but I just couldn't seem to shake them this time. Does that... does that make sense? Is that the kind of thing you usually help people with?

Well, um... maybe a little bit? I mean, I think most artists are a bit perfectionistic, right? It's good to be thorough. But... yeah, I guess I did do that a bit more than usual. 

I would, um... I'd end up rereading the label text a lot. I just had to keep doing it until it finally felt, I don't know, 'settled' in my mind. Even though a part of me completely knows it’s already fine and I’m really just losing valuable studio time. 

That’s probably just a normal part of the creative process when you're stressed, don't you think? I really hope that doesn't sound too strange.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, oh, they're definitely from me. It's just my own brain, you know? 

It's not like I'm hearing voices or feeling like someone else is putting thoughts in my head. Nothing scary or... or crazy like that. It's just me being a perfectionist, getting in my own way because I want the colors or the symmetry to be exactly right. It's just my own inner critic being a little too loud sometimes. 

That's an important distinction, isn't it? I just want to make sure I'm being clear that it's just my own normal thoughts, just... focused a bit too hard on my work. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for?

Um... well, I definitely don't *want* to have them. They're... they are pretty unwelcome. I mean, usually I just try to use my perfectionism as motivation, but... well, to be completely honest, the reason I actually made this appointment was because of something that happened recently. 

I was, um, working on a memorial piece for my aunt. And I just kept getting these really... awful, unwanted thoughts. It wasn't like my usual artistic process at all. I kept having this intense feeling that I was going to dishonor her somehow? Like... like if I made one wrong photo placement, or if I chose the wrong color, it would mean that I didn't love her enough. 

And I just... I couldn't brush them off like my normal creative doubts. They just stuck there, and it felt really terrible. But, um, grief can make people think weird things, right? I hope I'm explaining this okay... I just don't want you to think I'm completely losing my grip on reality or anything. Am I making sense?

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well, like I was mentioning a little bit ago... I really don't want to make it sound like a huge crisis, because I am still getting most of my work done. I promise I'm usually very reliable! 

But... I guess, yeah, if I'm being completely honest, it is causing some distress. Just because... well, as I said, the extra time I end up losing in the studio already cost me that grant deadline. And having to back out of the gallery talk that I had actually really wanted to do... that was pretty upsetting for me. I was really looking forward to it.

Um, gosh... I'm actually not entirely sure? I hope it's okay that I don't have an exact timeline for you. I'm sorry if that's not very helpful... I guess I just haven't really been keeping track.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... I think they're things I would normally think? Just, you know, because I'm so passionate about my work. I wouldn't say they feel foreign or strange, really. 

Like, it's totally normal for an artist to want to get the colors exactly right, or to fix a line that's a little wobbly. So the thoughts themselves are just... me being me. Just me wanting to be a good artist. I wouldn't want you to think I'm having bizarre thoughts or anything!

You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... no. No, I don't think so. It's really just focused on my artwork.
